CLASS: Competitive
SUBJECT: Indian Geography
CHAPTER: Rivers of India
LEVEL: Easy
Q: In which direction does the Narmada River mainly flow?
A: East
B: West
C: North
D: South
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: The Narmada flows westward through a rift valley and drains into the Arabian Sea.






CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Easy
Q: Who was Frederic Sorrieu?
A: A French artist
B: An Italian revolutionary
C: A German king
D: A British politician
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: Frederic Sorrieu was a French artist who prepared a series of prints in 1848 visualising his dream of a world made up of democratic and social republics.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Easy
Q: What did nationalism primarily promote in nineteenth-century Europe?
A: Loyalty only to monarchs
B: A sense of collective identity among people
C: The abolition of all cultural traditions
D: The expansion of colonial rule only
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Nationalism encouraged people to develop a shared identity and a sense of belonging to a nation, often based on common history, culture, language, or political aspirations.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which event marked the beginning of the French Revolution?
A: The Congress of Vienna
B: The unification of Germany
C: The fall of the Bastille in 1789
D: The formation of the Zollverein
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: The storming of the Bastille on 14 July 1789 became an important symbol of the French Revolution, which challenged absolute monarchy and promoted ideas of liberty and equality.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: What did the French revolutionaries declare about the nation?
A: The nation belonged exclusively to the king
B: Only the nobility could represent the nation
C: The nation should be ruled by foreign powers
D: Sovereignty belonged to the people
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: The French Revolution transferred the idea of sovereignty from the monarchy to the people. Citizens were increasingly regarded as members of a nation with common rights and responsibilities.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Easy
Q: Which political system existed in France before the French Revolution?
A: Absolute monarchy
B: Federal democracy
C: Communist government
D: Parliamentary republic
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: Before the French Revolution, France was ruled by an absolute monarch. The revolution challenged royal authority and the privileges enjoyed by the clergy and nobility.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who introduced the Civil Code of 1804 in France?
A: Louis XVI
B: Napoleon Bonaparte
C: Otto von Bismarck
D: Giuseppe Garibaldi
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Napoleon Bonaparte introduced the Civil Code of 1804, also known as the Napoleonic Code. It established equality before the law for male citizens and secured the right to property.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: What was the main purpose of the Treaty of Vienna of 1815?
A: To establish a united Germany
B: To introduce universal suffrage throughout Europe
C: To restore monarchies and create a conservative order
D: To abolish all European kingdoms
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: The Congress of Vienna, held in 1815, aimed to restore monarchies that had been overthrown during the Napoleonic period and establish a conservative political order in Europe.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who hosted the Congress of Vienna in 1815?
A: Napoleon Bonaparte
B: Giuseppe Mazzini
C: Otto von Bismarck
D: Duke Metternich
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: Austrian Chancellor Duke Metternich hosted the Congress of Vienna. He supported conservative policies designed to restore the traditional monarchies and suppress revolutionary movements.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Easy
Q: What was the Zollverein?
A: A customs union formed at the initiative of Prussia
B: A secret revolutionary society in Italy
C: A military alliance led by France
D: A peace treaty between Britain and Russia
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: The Zollverein was a customs union established in 1834 at the initiative of Prussia. It abolished tariff barriers among many German states and helped strengthen economic cooperation.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who founded Young Italy?
A: Count Cavour
B: Giuseppe Mazzini
C: Duke Metternich
D: Otto von Bismarck
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Giuseppe Mazzini founded Young Italy in 1831. He believed that Italy should become a unified, independent republic and inspired nationalist movements across Europe.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which group played a major role in promoting liberal nationalism in Europe?
A: Only hereditary monarchs
B: Only feudal landlords
C: Educated middle classes
D: Foreign colonial armies
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: Educated middle-class groups, including professionals, businesspeople, and intellectuals, played an important role in promoting liberal nationalism and constitutional government in Europe.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: What was the main objective of the Frankfurt Parliament of 1848?
A: To restore Napoleon to power
B: To establish Austrian control over all Europe
C: To divide Germany into smaller independent kingdoms permanently
D: To create a united German nation under a constitution
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: The Frankfurt Parliament was convened in 1848 to establish a united German nation with a constitutional monarchy. Its attempt failed when the Prussian king rejected the crown offered by the elected representatives.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Easy
Q: In which year was the Frankfurt Parliament convened?
A: 1848
B: 1815
C: 1834
D: 1871
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: The Frankfurt Parliament convened in May 1848 in St Paul's Church in Frankfurt. It represented an important attempt to unite the German states through constitutional and parliamentary means.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who played the leading role in the unification of Germany?
A: Giuseppe Mazzini
B: Otto von Bismarck
C: Count Camillo de Cavour
D: Napoleon III
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Otto von Bismarck, the chief minister of Prussia, led the process of German unification through diplomacy and wars involving Denmark, Austria, and France.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: In which year was the German Empire proclaimed?
A: 1848
B: 1861
C: 1871
D: 1815
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: The German Empire was proclaimed in January 1871 in the Hall of Mirrors at the Palace of Versailles. King William I of Prussia became the German Emperor.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Easy
Q: Which kingdom led the process of Italian unification?
A: Kingdom of Naples
B: Papal States
C: Kingdom of Sicily
D: Sardinia-Piedmont
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: Sardinia-Piedmont, ruled by the House of Savoy, played a leading role in Italian unification. Its chief minister, Count Cavour, used diplomacy and military alliances to advance the cause.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who was Count Camillo de Cavour?
A: Chief minister of Sardinia-Piedmont
B: The founder of Young Germany
C: The ruler of France during the Revolution of 1789
D: The host of the Congress of Vienna
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: Count Camillo de Cavour was the chief minister of Sardinia-Piedmont. He played an important role in Italian unification through diplomatic negotiations and an alliance with France against Austria.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who led the famous expedition of the Red Shirts in southern Italy?
A: Otto von Bismarck
B: Giuseppe Garibaldi
C: Duke Metternich
D: Napoleon Bonaparte
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Giuseppe Garibaldi led a volunteer force known as the Red Shirts in southern Italy in 1860. His campaign helped bring the Kingdom of the Two Sicilies under the control of the emerging Italian nation.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who was proclaimed king of united Italy in 1861?
A: Giuseppe Mazzini
B: Count Cavour
C: Victor Emmanuel II
D: Giuseppe Garibaldi
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: Victor Emmanuel II, the king of Sardinia-Piedmont, was proclaimed king of united Italy in 1861. Italian unification continued afterward, with Venetia joining in 1866 and Rome in 1870.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: History
CHAPTER: The Rise of Nationalism in Europe
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: What was the allegorical female figure representing France called?
A: Germania
B: Britannia
C: Marianne
D: Liberty Bell
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: Marianne was the allegorical female figure representing the French nation. She symbolised liberty and the Republic and appeared on coins, stamps, and public monuments. Germania represented the German nation.



CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Easy
Q: What is federalism?
A: A system in which power is divided between different levels of government
B: A system in which only the central government has power
C: A system without elections
D: A system in which judges make all decisions
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: Federalism is a system of government in which power is divided between a central authority and various constituent units of the country.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Easy
Q: Which of the following is a key feature of federalism?
A: One level of government controls all powers
B: Different levels of government have their own jurisdiction
C: State governments are abolished
D: The Constitution has no importance
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: In a federal system, different levels of government have their own constitutionally defined powers and responsibilities.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following countries is an example of a coming-together federation?
A: India
B: Belgium
C: United States of America
D: Spain
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: In a coming-together federation, independent states join together to form a larger country. The USA is an example.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following countries is an example of a holding-together federation?
A: United States of America
B: Switzerland
C: Australia
D: India
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: In a holding-together federation, a large country divides power between the central government and constituent units. India is an example.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Easy
Q: How many levels of government are there in India?
A: One
B: Two
C: Three
D: Four
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: India has three levels of government: Union Government, State Governments, and Local Governments.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following subjects belongs to the Union List?
A: Police
B: Defence
C: Agriculture
D: Irrigation
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Defence is a Union List subject because it concerns the security of the entire country.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following subjects belongs to the State List?
A: Foreign Affairs
B: Currency
C: Police
D: Banking
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: Police is a State List subject, and State Governments generally make laws concerning it.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following subjects belongs to the Concurrent List?
A: Defence
B: Foreign Affairs
C: Police
D: Education
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: Education is included in the Concurrent List, on which both Parliament and State Legislatures can make laws.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Who has the power to interpret the Constitution and settle disputes between different levels of government?
A: Election Commission
B: Judiciary
C: Finance Commission
D: Gram Sabha
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: The independent judiciary interprets the Constitution and helps resolve disputes regarding the constitutional division of powers.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Easy
Q: Which language is recognised as the official language of the Union Government of India?
A: English only
B: Hindi only without any other language being used
C: Hindi in Devanagari script
D: Sanskrit
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: Hindi in the Devanagari script is the official language of the Union. English continues to be used for official purposes as provided by law. India has no constitutionally declared national language.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: How many languages are listed in the Eighth Schedule of the Indian Constitution?
A: 18
B: 20
C: 22
D: 24
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: The Eighth Schedule of the Indian Constitution recognises 22 scheduled languages.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following best describes India's language policy?
A: Hindi has been imposed as the only language in every state
B: States have no role in language policy
C: The Constitution recognises only one Indian language
D: The Union has no declared national language, and several languages are recognised
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: India follows a flexible language policy. Hindi is the official language of the Union, English continues to be used for official purposes, and states can adopt their own official languages.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Easy
Q: What is decentralisation?
A: Concentrating all powers in the Union Government
B: Transferring powers to lower levels of government
C: Abolishing State Governments
D: Ending local elections
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Decentralisation means transferring powers and responsibilities from higher levels of government to lower levels, especially local governments.

A: 42nd Amendment
B: 44th Amendment
C: 73rd Amendment
D: 86th Amendment
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: The 73rd Constitutional Amendment gave constitutional status to rural local self-government institutions.

CLASS: 10

CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which Constitutional Amendment gave constitutional status to urban local bodies?
A: 52nd Amendment
B: 61st Amendment
C: 73rd Amendment
D: 74th Amendment
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: The 74th Constitutional Amendment gave constitutional status to urban local bodies, such as municipalities.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Easy
Q: Which institution is responsible for local self-government in villages?
A: Municipal Corporation
B: Gram Panchayat
C: Parliament
D: Supreme Court
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: The Gram Panchayat is a rural local government institution that looks after village-level development and local needs.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: What is the main purpose of creating linguistic states in India?
A: To divide the country into independent nations
B: To abolish regional languages
C: To recognise linguistic diversity and improve administration
D: To end the federal system
ANSWER: C
EXPLANATION: The creation of linguistic states recognised the linguistic identities of people and helped make administration more effective.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: What is one important feature of the Indian federal system?
A: State Governments have no constitutional powers
B: All states must use the same regional language
C: The Constitution can be changed by the Union Government alone in every matter
D: The Constitution divides powers between the Union and State Governments
ANSWER: D
EXPLANATION: The Constitution specifies the distribution of powers between the Union and State Governments. Certain constitutional amendments require ratification by at least half of the State Legislatures.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Which of the following helped strengthen federalism in India?
A: Abolishing all State Governments
B: Respecting the autonomy of State Governments and improving Centre-State relations
C: Ending regional political parties
D: Giving all powers to local governments
ANSWER: B
EXPLANATION: Respect for State autonomy, democratic politics, and improved Centre-State relations have contributed to the strengthening of federalism in India.

CLASS: 10
SUBJECT: Civics
CHAPTER: Federalism
LEVEL: Moderate
Q: Why is federalism considered a form of power-sharing?
A: Because it divides power among different levels of government
B: Because it gives all power to one individual
C: Because it eliminates the Constitution
D: Because it prevents people from participating in government
ANSWER: A
EXPLANATION: Federalism distributes power among different levels of government, allowing each level to exercise authority within its assigned jurisdiction.
